\doxysubsection{Detailed Description}
Provides the function definitions for the system, clocking, interrupts and watchdog timer API. 

\begin{DoxyVersion}{Version}
6.\+0
\end{DoxyVersion}


\begin{DoxyNote}{Note}
Documentation of the API\textquotesingle{}s in this file is located in the System Reference Guide provided with PSoC Creator.

The macro that evaluates the expression and if it is false (evaluates to 0) then the processor is halted.

This macro is evaluated unless NDEBUG is defined.

If NDEBUG is defined, then no code is generated for this macro. NDEBUG is defined by default for a Release build setting and not defined for a Debug build setting.


\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em expr} & Logical expression. Asserts if false. \\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}

Blocks for microseconds.

Note\+: Cy\+Delay has been implemented with the instruction cache assumed enabled. When instruction cache is disabled on PSo\+C5, Cy\+Delay\+Us will be two times larger. Ex\+: With instruction cache disabled Cy\+Delay\+Us(100) would result in about 200us delay instead of 100us.


\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}

\hline

\textbackslash{}sideeffect Cy\+Delay\+US has been implemented with the instruction cache assumed enabled. When the instruction cache is disabled on PSoC 5, Cy\+Delay\+Us will be two times larger. For example, with the instruction cache disabled Cy\+Delay\+Us(100) would result in about 200 us delay instead of 100 us.

If the bus clock frequency is a small non-\/integer number, the actual delay can be up to twice as long as the nominal value. The actual delay cannot be shorter than the nominal one. \Hypertarget{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_a1b36566c56e66f6ad2e4c2d8cf17a028}\index{CyLib.h@{CyLib.h}!CyDisableInts@{CyDisableInts}}

\Hypertarget{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_a1489ff02ca90e7cf2a76205d619ad394}\index{CyLib.h@{CyLib.h}!CyFlushCache@{CyFlushCache}}
\index{CyFlushCache@{CyFlushCache}!CyLib.h@{CyLib.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{CyFlushCache()}{CyFlushCache()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_a1489ff02ca90e7cf2a76205d619ad394} 
void Cy\+Flush\+Cache (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Call this API after a flash row erase/write operation to invalidate or flush any of that particular flash region content already present in the cache. After a cache flush operation, any access to that flash region after the erase/write operation would reload the cache with the modified data from the flash region. If the flash region update involves multiple flash row write operations, then the flushing of the cache can be done once at the end of the operation as long as the flash data would not be accessed in the middle of the multiple row update process. Else, flush the cache after every flash row write. \Hypertarget{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_a9f2d649e88e1baaeb471e2453aa53d89}\index{CyLib.h@{CyLib.h}!CyGetUniqueId@{CyGetUniqueId}}

void Cy\+Get\+Unique\+Id (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{uint32 \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{unique\+Id}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Returns the 64-\/bit unique ID of the device. The uniqueness of the number is guaranteed for 10 years due to the die lot number having a cycle life of 10 years and even after 10 years, the probability of getting two identical numbers is very small.


\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em unique\+Id} & The pointer to a two element 32-\/bit unsigned integer array. Returns the 64-\/bit unique ID of the device by loading them into the integer array pointed to by unique\+Id. \\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}

\textbackslash{}sideeffect If wait is enabled\+: This function uses the Fast Time Wheel to time the wait. Any other use of the Fast Time Wheel will be stopped during the period of this function and then restored. This function also uses the 100 KHz ILO. No changes to the setup of the ILO, Fast Time Wheel, Central Time Wheel or Once Per Second interrupt may be made by interrupt routines during the period of this function execution. void Cy\+Sys\+Tick\+Init (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Initializes the callback addresses with pointers to NULL, associates the Sys\+Tick system vector with the function that is responsible for calling registered callback functions, configures Sys\+Tick timer to generate interrupt every 1 ms.

\textbackslash{}sideeffect Clears Sys\+Tick count flag if it was set.

The 1 ms interrupt interval is configured based on the frequency determined by PSoC Creator at build time. If System clock frequency is changed in runtime, the \doxylink{codegentemp_2_cy_lib_8c_a22accda26c0d6d8d1efca71f6fdcf5ed}{Cy\+Delay\+Freq()} with the appropriate parameter should be called. \Hypertarget{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_a1cd50582bd86a12d2e4e9025da3ddf59}\index{CyLib.h@{CyLib.h}!CySysTickSetCallback@{CySysTickSetCallback}}

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_a1cd50582bd86a12d2e4e9025da3ddf59}

This function allows up to five user-\/defined interrupt service routine functions to be associated with the Sys\+Tick interrupt. These are specified through the use of pointers to the function.

To set a custom callback function without the overhead of the system provided one, use Cy\+Int\+Set\+Sys\+Vector(\+CY\+\_\+\+INT\+\_\+\+SYSTICK\+\_\+\+IRQN, cyisraddress $<$address$>$), where $<$address$>$ is address of the custom defined interrupt service routine. Note\+: a custom callback function overrides the system defined callback functions.


\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em number} & The number of the callback function addresses to be set. The valid range is from 0 to 4.\\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}
void(\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}\+Callback\+Function(void)\+: A pointer to the function that will be associated with the Sys\+Tick ISR for the specified number.

\begin{DoxyReturn}{Returns}
Returns the address of the previous callback function. The NULL is returned if the specified address in not set.
\end{DoxyReturn}
\textbackslash{}sideeffect The registered callback functions will be executed in the interrupt. \Hypertarget{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_a8de4d3094b469bd3a3d79639cd0de4f0}\index{CyLib.h@{CyLib.h}!CySysTickSetClockSource@{CySysTickSetClockSource}}

CY\+\_\+\+SYS\+\_\+\+SYST\+\_\+\+CSR\+\_\+\+CLK\+\_\+\+SRC\+\_\+\+LFCLK Sys\+Tick is clocked by the low frequency clock (ILO 100 KHz for PSoC 5LP, and LFCLK for PSoC 4).\\

Note The associated interrupt enable/disable state is not changed by the function. The pending interrupt status is not cleared. The Interrupt component API should be used to manipulate with the associated interrupts. \Hypertarget{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_ac37bddbef25b3fc8b314edaa8a4172ba}\index{CyLib.h@{CyLib.h}!CyVdLvAnalogEnable@{CyVdLvAnalogEnable}}

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_ac37bddbef25b3fc8b314edaa8a4172ba} 
void Cy\+Vd\+Lv\+Analog\+Enable (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{uint8}]{reset}{, }\item[{uint8}]{threshold}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Sets the voltage trip level, enables the output of the analog low-\/voltage monitor, and optionally configures voltage monitor to reset device upon the low-\/voltage event instead of generating an interrupt.

Note The associated interrupt enable/disable state is not changed by the function. The Interrupt component API should be used to register the interrupt service routine and to enable/disable associated interrupt.


\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em reset} & Enables device reset on analog low-\/voltage event\+: Zero -\/ Interrupt on analog low-\/voltage event Non-\/zero -\/ Reset on analog low-\/voltage event\\
\hline
{\em threshold} & Sets the trip point of the analog low-\/voltage monitoring circuit in steps of approximately 250 mV in range from 1.\+70 V (0x00) to 5.\+45 V (0x0F). For example, the trip point is set to 1.\+80 V when value of the threshold parameter is 0x04. Please refer to the device TRM for the exact trip voltage values.\\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}
Side Effects and Restrictions\+: The voltage resets are momentary. When a voltage reset (analog/digital low-\/voltage and analog high-\/voltage) occurs, the RESET\+\_\+\+CR1 and RESET\+\_\+\+CR3 registers are restored to their default values. This means that the voltage monitor circuit is no longer enabled and the device exits reset. If the supply is below the trip level and firmware enables the voltage reset functionality, the device will reset again. This will continue as long as the supply is below the trip level or as long as the user enables the reset functionality of the voltage monitor functionality.

When any voltage reset occurs, the RESET\+\_\+\+SR0 and RESET\+\_\+\+SR2 status registers are cleared. This means that analog low-\/voltage, digital low-\/voltage and analog high-\/voltage status bits are not persistent across any voltage reset. \Hypertarget{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_a5a4e655b5519ba9333b327711bc74de0}\index{CyLib.h@{CyLib.h}!CyVdLvDigitDisable@{CyVdLvDigitDisable}}

void Cy\+Vd\+Lv\+Digit\+Disable (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Disables the digital low-\/voltage monitor, turns off device reset upon the digital low-\/voltage event, and clears the associated persistent status bit.

Note The associated interrupt enable/disable state is not changed by the function. The pending interrupt status is not cleared. The Interrupt component API should be used to manipulate with the associated interrupts. \Hypertarget{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_ab8214aa2f0f6beccf8417ad34a72470d}\index{CyLib.h@{CyLib.h}!CyVdLvDigitEnable@{CyVdLvDigitEnable}}

For example, the trip point is set to 1.\+80 V when the threshold parameter value is 0x04. Refer to the device TRM for the exact trip voltage values.\\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}
Side Effects and Restrictions\+: The voltage resets are momentary. When a voltage reset (analog/digital low-\/voltage and analog high-\/voltage) occurs, the RESET\+\_\+\+CR1 and RESET\+\_\+\+CR3 registers are restored to their default values. This means that the voltage monitor circuit is no longer enabled and the device exits reset. If the supply is below the trip level and firmware enables the voltage reset functionality, the device will reset again. This will continue as long as the supply is below the trip level or as long as the user enables the reset functionality of the voltage monitor functionality.

When any voltage reset occurs, the RESET\+\_\+\+SR0 and RESET\+\_\+\+SR2 status registers are cleared. This means that analog low-\/voltage, digital low-\/voltage and analog high-\/voltage status bits are not persistent across any voltage reset. \Hypertarget{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_a071a5639ef1122ff6f232bb0b8545170}\index{CyLib.h@{CyLib.h}!CyVdRealTimeStatus@{CyVdRealTimeStatus}}

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_a071a5639ef1122ff6f232bb0b8545170} 
uint8 Cy\+Vd\+Real\+Time\+Status (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Reads the real-\/time voltage detection status bits in the RESET\+\_\+\+SR2 register. The bits are set to 1 by the voltage monitor circuit when the supply is outside the detector’s trip point, and set to 0 when the supply is inside the trip point.

\begin{DoxyReturn}{Returns}
Status of the LVID, LVIA, and HVIA bits in the RESET\+\_\+\+SR2 register. Define Definition CY\+\_\+\+VD\+\_\+\+LVID Real-\/time status of digital LVI. CY\+\_\+\+VD\+\_\+\+LVIA Real-\/time status of analog LVI. CY\+\_\+\+VD\+\_\+\+HVIA Real-\/time status of analog HVI.
\end{DoxyReturn}
Side Effects and Restrictions\+: When an LVI reset occurs, the RESET\+\_\+\+SR2 status registers are cleared. This means that the voltage detection status bits are not persistent across an LVI reset and cannot be used to determine a reset source. \Hypertarget{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_a678a040ec217c51fb1e9075aa3f818d6}\index{CyLib.h@{CyLib.h}!CyVdStickyStatus@{CyVdStickyStatus}}

uint8 Cy\+Vd\+Sticky\+Status (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{uint8}]{mask}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Reads and clears the voltage detection status bits in the RESET\+\_\+\+SR0 register. The bits are set to 1 by the voltage monitor circuit when the supply is outside the detector trip point. They stay set to 1 until they are read or a POR / LVI / PRES reset occurs. This function uses a shadow register, so only the bits passed in the parameter will be cleared in the shadow register.


\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em mask} & Bits in the RESET\+\_\+\+SR0 shadow register to clear and return. Define Definition CY\+\_\+\+VD\+\_\+\+LVID Persistent status of digital LVI. CY\+\_\+\+VD\+\_\+\+LVIA Persistent status of analog LVI. CY\+\_\+\+VD\+\_\+\+HVIA Persistent status of analog HVI.\\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}
\begin{DoxyReturn}{Returns}
Status. Same enumerated bit values as used for the mask parameter. A zero is returned for bits not used in the mask parameter.
\end{DoxyReturn}
Side Effects and Restrictions\+: When an LVI reset occurs, the RESET\+\_\+\+SR0 status registers are cleared. This means that the voltage detection status bits are not persistent across an LVI reset and cannot be used to determine a reset source. \Hypertarget{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_a162b380c11dc33427fd7286d4eef0166}\index{CyLib.h@{CyLib.h}!CyWdtClear@{CyWdtClear}}

void Cy\+Wdt\+Clear (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Clears (feeds) the watchdog timer. \Hypertarget{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_a258ffba3305bdba4ac83c309e7bdbc52}\index{CyLib.h@{CyLib.h}!CyWdtStart@{CyWdtStart}}
\index{CyWdtStart@{CyWdtStart}!CyLib.h@{CyLib.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{CyWdtStart()}{CyWdtStart()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_a258ffba3305bdba4ac83c309e7bdbc52} 
void Cy\+Wdt\+Start (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{uint8}]{ticks}{, }\item[{uint8}]{lp\+Mode}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Enables the watchdog timer.

The timer is configured for the specified count interval, the central timewheel is cleared, the setting for the low power mode is configured and the watchdog timer is enabled.

Once enabled the watchdog cannot be disabled. The watchdog counts each time the Central Time Wheel (CTW) reaches the period specified. The watchdog must be cleared using the \doxylink{codegentemp_2_cy_lib_8c_a162b380c11dc33427fd7286d4eef0166}{Cy\+Wdt\+Clear()} function before three ticks of the watchdog timer occur. The CTW is free running, so this will occur after between 2 and 3 timer periods elapse.

PSo\+C5\+: The watchdog timer should not be used during sleep modes. Since the WDT cannot be disabled after it is enabled, the WDT timeout period can be set to be greater than the sleep wakeup period, then feed the dog on each wakeup from Sleep.


\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em ticks} & One of the four available timer periods. Once WDT enabled, the interval cannot be changed. CYWDT\+\_\+2\+\_\+\+TICKS -\/ 4 -\/ 6 ms CYWDT\+\_\+16\+\_\+\+TICKS -\/ 32 -\/ 48 ms CYWDT\+\_\+128\+\_\+\+TICKS -\/ 256 -\/ 384 ms CYWDT\+\_\+1024\+\_\+\+TICKS -\/ 2.\+048 -\/ 3.\+072 s\\
\hline
{\em lp\+Mode} & Low power mode configuration. This parameter is ignored for PSoC 5. The WDT always acts as if CYWDT\+\_\+\+LPMODE\+\_\+\+NOCHANGE is passed.\\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}
CYWDT\+\_\+\+LPMODE\+\_\+\+NOCHANGE -\/ No Change CYWDT\+\_\+\+LPMODE\+\_\+\+MAXINTER -\/ Switch to longest timer mode during low power mode CYWDT\+\_\+\+LPMODE\+\_\+\+DISABLED -\/ Disable WDT during low power mode

\textbackslash{}sideeffect PSo\+C5\+: The ILO 1 KHz must be enabled for proper WDT operation. Stopping the ILO 1 k\+Hz could break the active WDT functionality. \Hypertarget{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_ae8238d6f957458a0137ff17cb35379e7}\index{CyLib.h@{CyLib.h}!CyXTAL\_32KHZ\_ReadStatus@{CyXTAL\_32KHZ\_ReadStatus}}

Enables the fault recovery circuit which will switch to the IMO in the case of a fault in the megahertz crystal circuit. The crystal must be up and running with the XERR bit at 0, before calling this function to prevent an immediate fault switchover. This function is not available for PSo\+C5. \Hypertarget{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_a18c8e9e93ae85143624b103a2ccd5888}\index{CyLib.h@{CyLib.h}!CyXTAL\_ReadStatus@{CyXTAL\_ReadStatus}}

\Hypertarget{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_a74e124d28c39efa187ea34eda93858d2}\index{CyLib.h@{CyLib.h}!CyXTAL\_Start@{CyXTAL\_Start}}

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{CyXTAL\_Start()}{CyXTAL\_Start()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_a74e124d28c39efa187ea34eda93858d2} 
cystatus Cy\+XTAL\+\_\+\+Start (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{uint8}]{wait}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Enables the megahertz crystal.

PSoC 3\+: Waits until the XERR bit is low (no error) for a millisecond or until the number of milliseconds specified by the wait parameter has expired.


\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em wait} & Valid range \mbox{[}0-\/255\mbox{]}. This is the timeout value in milliseconds. The appropriate value is crystal specific.\\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}
\begin{DoxyReturn}{Returns}
CYRET\+\_\+\+SUCCESS -\/ Completed successfully CYRET\+\_\+\+TIMEOUT -\/ Timeout occurred without detecting a low value on XERR.
\end{DoxyReturn}
Side Effects and Restrictions\+: If wait is enabled (non-\/zero wait). Uses the Fast Timewheel to time the wait. Any other use of the Fast Timewheel (FTW) will be stopped during the period of this function and then restored.

Uses the 100KHz ILO. If not enabled, this function will enable the 100KHz ILO for the period of this function. No changes to the setup of the ILO, Fast Timewheel, Central Timewheel or Once Per Second interrupt may be made by interrupt routines during the period of this function.

The current operation of the ILO, Central Timewheel and Once Per Second interrupt are maintained during the operation of this function provided the reading of the Power Manager Interrupt Status Register is only done using the \doxylink{codegentemp_2cy_pm_8c_a7f6c3c26c960cc2a69aa7d9a409875ff}{Cy\+Pm\+Read\+Status()} function. \Hypertarget{_generated___source_2_p_so_c5_2_cy_lib_8h_abc31c6a620cad2b290d1220ca98252b0}\index{CyLib.h@{CyLib.h}!CyXTAL\_Stop@{CyXTAL\_Stop}}
